- **chimeric-antigen-receptor-transcript-alignment**: Use when scRNA-seq libraries incompletely capture CAR transgenes and you need per-cell CAR vs wildtype classification from targeted long-read amplicon FASTQ (e.g. ONT reads of 10x 5' cDNA). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/chimeric-antigen-receptor-transcript-alignment))
- **chimeric-aware-rna-seq-alignment**: Use when RNA-seq FASTQ reads from a tumor sample must be aligned for fusion detection (Arriba) or for RNA-side variant callers (transIndel, ScanExitron) and chimeric events such as gene fusions must not be missed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/chimeric-aware-rna-seq-alignment))
- **chimeric-bin-taxonomic-discordance-filtering**: Use when a MAG catalogue (e.g., 19,542 VMGC prokaryotic FASTA genomes) has per-genome CheckM2 Completeness/Contamination estimates and bins must be screened for taxonomic discordance (chimerism) prior to quality classification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/chimeric-bin-taxonomic-discordance-filtering))
- **chimeric-contig-breakpoint-splitting**: Use when a de novo draft assembly (e.g. Falcon/Canu contigs) may contain misjoins, and raw Illumina short reads plus PacBio/ONT long reads are available to detect CSE breakpoints before scaffolding with Hi-C or optical maps. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/chimeric-contig-breakpoint-splitting))
- **chimeric-contig-classification-and-count-tabulation**: Use when you have a de novo assembled transcriptome (e.g., Trinity contigs) plus a reference transcript set and need to quantify how many chimeric vs. correct vs. unidentified contigs a filter removed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/chimeric-contig-classification-and-count-tabulation))
- **chimeric-read-artifact-modeling**: Use when detecting gene fusions from Smart-Seq scRNA-seq FASTQ data where PCR amplification produces artificial chimeric reads that inflate false-positive fusion candidates. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/chimeric-read-artifact-modeling))
- **chimeric-read-classification**: Use when building or evaluating the false-positive filter of a targeted fusion-detection pipeline that must distinguish true fusion reads from artificial chimeric reads in paired-end RNA-seq data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/chimeric-read-classification))
- **chimeric-read-correlation-analysis**: Use when STAR alignment logs from RNA-seq samples grouped by tissue show anomalous unmapped ('too short') rates and you need to test whether chimeric reads co-occur with unmappable reads, e.g. to diagnose genome-build or annotation-driven mapping failures in one tissue. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/chimeric-read-correlation-analysis))
- **chimeric-read-detection**: Use when running DBG2OLC overlap/layout on long erroneous PacBio or Nanopore reads with &gt;10x coverage, where chimeric reads and spurious contig identifiers would corrupt the backbone layout. - **chimeric-read-filtering**: Use when a Nanopore BAM with BC, U8 and GE tags is about to be collapsed into per-UMI consensus sequences and you must drop chimeric (clipped) reads and MAPQ=0 alignments beforehand. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/chimeric-read-filtering-long-reads))
- **chimeric-read-local-alignment**: Use when running Anchored-fusion on paired-end RNA-seq (FASTQ) data and you have candidate chimeric reads whose mates fail to map to the anchored target gene, requiring precise fusion-partner localization and breakpoint resolution. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/chimeric-read-local-alignment))
- **chimeric-read-negative-sample-generation**: Use when retraining the HVLD false-positive filter of Anchored-fusion on a new RNA-seq dataset and dataset-specific artificial fusion reads are needed as negative training samples alongside PCAWG positive samples. - **chimeric-read-removal**: Use when running DBG2OLC overlap/layout on raw long (PacBio/3GS) reads for hybrid assembly and the long-read set may contain chimeric molecules, especially with &gt;10x long-read coverage. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/chimeric-read-removal))
- **chimeric-read-splitting**: Use when demultiplexing long reads (e.g. Ebrahimi et al. 2022 simulated FASTQ) that may contain more than one barcode construct per read, and each detected construct must be reported separately. - **chimeric-read-supporting-evidence-quantification**: Use when benchmarking fusion-gene detectors on simulated paired-end FASTQ inputs and you need per-tool counts of spanning reads, split reads, and lowest coverage at which BCR-ABL1, CIC-DUX4, or DUX4-IGH is detected. - **chimeric-rna-segment-type-annotation**: Use when input is a hyb-format hybrid (chimeric RNA) file from miRNA/target ligation methods such as CLASH/qCLASH and records need seg1_type/seg2_type and miRNA_seg annotations before quantification or filtering. - **chimeric-spectra-classification-model-training**: Use when when your DDA-mode LC-MS/MS data exhibits chimeric spectra patterns that differ systematically from the reference training set used in DNMS2Purifier, or when you wish to optimize purification sensitivity/specificity for your particular instrument, ionization method, or sample matrix.
